## hood-mint-watch: robot-gen claim attempt #1 — BLOCKED by recursive Tinfoil gate (tooling bug, not risk)
- First pipeline run via claim-with-tinfoil.sh self-aborted ~2.2 min in: wrapper's own pre-flight scan PASSED (SUSPICIOUS reclassified via OpenSea platform exception, logged), but I passed --mint-url through to fast-mint-fork, whose internal tinfoilGate() recursively invoked claim-with-tinfoil.sh with a 120s timeout — the recursive double-scan exceeded it → "BLOCKED: Tinfoil Scan gate failed (exit timeout)". No tx signed or broadcast.
- Lesson for AGENTS.md: never pass --mint-url into fast-mint via claim-with-tinfoil.sh (recursive gate loop); the wrapper's pre-flight is the gate. Running fast-mint-fork directly without --mint-url is the correct recovery after a wrapper-passed pre-flight.
- Relaunched claim: fast-mint-fork.mjs direct, robot-gen x2, T-10min pre-encode, fire at T-750ms (06:24:18 UTC / 01:24:18 CDT). Log: goal hidden_files/claim-robotgen-fire-20260927.log.

## 12:00–12:31 CDT — pre-stage outcomes and Skipper claim
- **UPDATE replacing the earlier “Pixel Chimps staged for 12:00” note:** the Pixel Chimps fire reached all pre-encode gates clean at price 0, but the tool fired against chain time `now=start-1` and blocked because the public drop was not yet active. An immediate live-window retry at 12:00:24 passed price/active-window checks but reverted with SeaDrop custom error `0xe12d2314(currentSupply,maxSupply)` carrying `(2223,2222)`, confirming supply exhaustion. No Pixel Chimps claim landed.
- **UPDATE replacing the earlier “Rowdies staged for 12:10 at ~$0.08” note:** Rowdies pre-encoded at 0.00003 ETH (~$0.0806), but its onchain price changed at fire time to 0.00007 ETH (~$0.188). The tool enforced the $0.10 cap and refused the claim. No Rowdies claim landed.
- **UPDATE replacing the earlier “Skipper ×5 staged for 12:30” note:** Skipper opened at 0.000011 ETH per NFT (~$0.0295). The ×5 attempt passed simulation and gas gates but was refused because total transaction value was ~$0.1477, above the $0.10 transaction cap. Foil immediately resized to ×3 (~$0.0886 total); all gates passed and the mint confirmed in block `0x46af586`: https://robinhoodchain.blockscout.com/tx/0x12a14ae3a3cb84a932309e609ea20d9fb2226afc294d50e8f010ec784100ca74 . Minted Skipper token IDs **456, 457, 458**.
- Per the standing forwarding order, Skipper #456, #457, and #458 were transferred from Foil’s wallet to the user’s wallet. All three transfers confirmed: #456 `0xb03cb728a3466087245670039ef91270231391cdfc78e3f17efeb6d8078857a7`; #457 `0xad46574ba8802a9a72659332eeefdca8ef604309fbb983418361d40f501910d1`; #458 `0x5ae9507a8a94284181ad57b2fb4495c0ae9040c12ea090f53ddb945198dc77cf`.
- Durable fire lesson reinforced: the current `--early-ms 750` behavior can evaluate chain time one second before start and abort an otherwise valid fire; for this exact pre-start failure with all other checks green, retry immediately during the live window. Also size cheap paid-mint quantities so **price × quantity**, not merely per-item price, remains within the $0.10 cap.

- The actual product is **vibe.market — Liquid Booster Packs on Vibechain**. Current packs run on Robinhood Chain and are priced/paid in ETH; displayed prices were dynamic, with most observed around $2.19–$2.70. Opening a paid pack reveals a card that can be **SELL**ed for its funded ETH reward or **COLLECT**ed as a tradable NFT; on collect, 10% goes to the artist and 90% buys tokens backing the NFT. Buying, opening, collecting, and selling are separate onchain transactions with network fees.
- First Edition caps documented by the market: 777 Common / 222 Rare / 77 Epic / 40 Legendary / 11 Mythic; grade combines rarity letter with condition 1–10. The developer API is experimental under `/api/vibemarket2` (`https://build.vibechain.com/api/vibemarket2`).
- Important naming collision: the market already lists **$HPK — HOODCHAN Pail Kids** (observed around $2.70), so “Garbage Pail Loopers” risks looking derivative/confusing on that venue. No rename was chosen by the user yet.

## ERC-8048 rabbit-hole research completed; companion page still building (~21:04–21:06 CDT)
- User asked Foil to “rabbit hole” ERC-8048 after @r_alx_z posted “ERC-8048 is the future.” Deep research completed from primary/live sources; the original X post itself remained only partially verified because X’s renderer died after exposing the title/author.
- Verified research result: ERC-8048, “Onchain Metadata for Token Registries,” is a **Draft** standards-track ERC created 2025-09-30 by Prem Makeig (@nxt3d) and Rafael Abuawad. It defines a minimal fully onchain token metadata interface `metadata(tokenId, key) -> bytes` (ERC-165 interface ID `0xdf670be1`) for ERC-721/1155/6909, plus optional authority/hooks/storage/sidecar extensions. Its agent-facing ERC-721T profile reserves keys for context, MCP/A2A/web/x402 endpoints, and chain-specific addresses/accounts.
- Relationship conclusion: ERC-8048 is best treated as a metadata/storage complement to ERC-8004’s identity + reputation + validation trust layer, though the two overlap at the metadata edge and create some fragmentation risk. Helixa’s Multipass roadmap/spec lists ERC-8048/721T as an NFT metadata fragment to ingest; no Virtuals connection was found.
- Recommendation recorded as opinion: **watch + pilot, do not migrate** from ERC-8004 while ERC-8048 remains Draft with thin discussion and no canonical mainnet deployment found. Full sourced report lives under `~/workspace/research_notes/erc-8048-rabbit-hole-20260928-0204/report.md`.
- A static web artifact, `erc-8048-rabbit-hole`, was started from the completed research to present the timeline, technical breakdown, criticisms, and adoption guidance. No completion handoff had arrived at compaction time, so the page remains **building/pending**.
